\displaystyle\asymp s^{4}\bigg(\|\bm{U}\|_{2,\infty}^{4}\frac{\kappa^{4}r}{nL^{4}}+\|\bm{U}\|_{2,\infty}^{8}n^{2}r^{2}\kappa^{8}\bigg).

Thus, we see that the term in the parentheses is
o(1)
whenever (
11
) holds.
This completes the proof.∎

B.2.1
Proof of
Lemma

11

Proof.

First, we note that

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}^{\top}\sum_{l}\bm{N}^{(l)}\bm{S}^{(l)}\bm{U}((\bm{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}}^{\top})^{-1})\|_{F}\leq\frac{\sqrt{r}}{L\lambda^{2}}\|\bm{U}^{\top}\sum_{l}\bm{N}^{(l)}\bm{S}^{(l)}\|.

We now bound the quantity
\bm{U}^{\top}\sum_{l}\bm{N}^{(l)}\bm{S}^{(l)}
. The proof is markedly similar to the proof of
Lemma

1
. Modifying the proof, by taking a net over the set of vectors of dimension
r
we can show that

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}^{\top}\sum_{l}\bm{N}^{(l)}\bm{S}^{(l)}\|\leq Cs\sigma\sqrt{L}\lambda_{\max}

with probability at least
1-\exp(cr-s^{2})
. Therefore, taking
s\asymp\sqrt{r}+t
shows that

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}^{\top}\sum_{l}\bm{N}^{(l)}\bm{S}^{(l)}\|\leq C\sigma(\sqrt{r}+t)\sqrt{L}\lambda_{\max}

with probability at least
1-\exp(-ct^{2})
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

7

We now provide the following result, which provides consistent estimators of the asymptotic mean and variance.

Lemma 12
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

5
, with probability at least
1-\exp(-cn)
it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{2}\bigg|{\sf Tr}\bigg((\bm{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}}^{\top})^{-1}\bigg)-{\sf Tr}\bigg(\big(\bm{\mathcal{\widehat{R}}}\bm{\mathcal{\widehat{R}}}^{\top}\big)^{-1}\bigg)\bigg|}{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{n/2}\|(\bm{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}}^{\top})^{-1}\|_{F}}

\displaystyle=o(1);

\displaystyle\bigg|1-\frac{\|\big(\bm{\mathcal{\widehat{R}}}\bm{\mathcal{\widehat{R}}}^{\top}\big)^{-1}\|_{F}}{\|(\bm{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}}^{\top})^{-1}\|_{F}}\bigg|

\displaystyle=o(1).

Proof.

See
Section

B.3.1
.
∎

We are now prepared to prove
Theorem

7
.

Proof of
Theorem

7
.

First, we note that
Theorem

5
implies that

\displaystyle\frac{\|\sin\Theta(\bm{\widehat{U}},\bm{U})\|_{F}^{2}-\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{2}{\sf Tr}\big((\bm{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}}^{\top})^{-1}\big)}{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{n/2}\|(\bm{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}}^{\top})^{-1}\|_{F}}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

Thus, it holds that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\|\sin\Theta(\bm{\widehat{U}},\bm{U})\|_{F}^{2}\in\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{2}{\sf Tr}\big((\bm{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}}^{\top})^{-1}\big)\pm z_{\alpha/2}\sigma^{2}\sqrt{n/2}\|(\bm{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}}^{\top})^{-1}\|_{F}\bigg\}\geq 1-\alpha.

We also have that the interval above is of length of order at most
\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{nr}}{L\lambda^{2}}\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}nr}{L\lambda^{2}}\times\frac{1}{\sqrt{nr}}
, uniformly over all
\mathcal{P}(\lambda)
where
\lambda
satisfies
\lambda\gg\frac{nr^{2}\kappa^{4}}{\sqrt{L}}
.
Define

\displaystyle\mu:=\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{2}{\sf Tr}\big((\bm{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}}^{\top})^{-1}\big);

\displaystyle\qquad\widehat{\mu}=\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{2}{\sf Tr}\big(\big(\bm{\mathcal{\widehat{R}}}\bm{\mathcal{\widehat{R}}}^{\top}\big)^{-1}\big);

\displaystyle\nu=\sigma^{2}\sqrt{n/2}\|(\bm{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}}^{\top})^{-1}\|_{F};

\displaystyle\qquad\widehat{\nu}=\sigma^{2}\sqrt{n/2}\|\big(\bm{\mathcal{\widehat{R}}}\bm{\mathcal{\widehat{R}}}^{\top}\big)^{-1}\|_{F}.

Define

\displaystyle Z_{n}:=\frac{\|\sin\Theta(\bm{\widehat{U}},\bm{U})\|_{F}^{2}-\mu}{\nu};\qquad a_{n}=\frac{\widehat{\mu}-\mu}{\nu};\qquad b_{n}=\frac{\widehat{\nu}}{\nu}.

Then we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\|\sin\Theta(\bm{\widehat{U}},\bm{U})\|_{F}^{2}\in\widehat{{\sf CI}_{\alpha}}\bigg\}=\mathbb{P}\big\{|Z_{n}-a_{n}|\leq z_{\alpha/2}b_{n}\big\}.

Slutsky’s theorem and
Lemma

12
implies that
Z_{n}-a_{n}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
and
z_{\alpha/2}b_{n}\xrightarrow{p}z_{\alpha/2}
. Furthermore, we have that
|a_{n}|=o(1),|b_{n}-1|=o(1)
. Then we have that

\displaystyle\liminf\mathbb{P}\big\{|Z_{n}-a_{n}|\leq z_{\alpha/2}b_{n}\big\}\geq\liminf\mathbb{P}\big\{|Z_{n}|\leq z_{\alpha/2}(1-o(1))-o(1)\big\}\to 1-\alpha.

Thus we have the desired coverage. The bound on the length follows immediately from
Lemma

12
.
∎

B.3.1
Proof of
Lemma

12

Proof of
Lemma

12
.

From
Lemma

6
, we have that

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{2}{\sf Tr}\bigg((\bm{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}}^{\top})^{-1}\bigg)-\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{2}{\sf Tr}\bigg(\big(\bm{\mathcal{\widehat{R}}}\bm{\mathcal{\widehat{R}}}^{\top}\big)^{-1}\bigg)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{r}\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{2}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{l}\big(\bm{\widehat{R}}^{(l)}\big)^{2}\bigg)^{-1}-\mathcal{O}^{\top}\bigg(\sum_{l}\big(\bm{R}^{(l)}\big)^{2}\bigg)^{-1}\mathcal{O}\bigg\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma\kappa\sqrt{nr}}{\lambda\sqrt{L}}\frac{\kappa^{2}\sigma^{2}n\sqrt{r}}{\lambda^{2}L}+\frac{\sigma\kappa\sqrt{nr}}{\lambda\sqrt{L}}\frac{\sigma^{4}n^{2}r^{2}}{\lambda^{4}L^{3/2}}+\frac{\sigma^{4}nr^{2}}{\lambda^{4}L^{3/2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\sigma^{4}n^{2}r^{2}}{\lambda^{4}L^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{4}r^{2}n^{3/2}}{\lambda^{4}L^{3/2}}+\frac{\sigma\kappa\sqrt{nr}}{\lambda\sqrt{L}}\frac{\sigma^{3}r^{3/2}n^{2}\kappa}{\lambda^{3}L^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{3}n^{3/2}r\kappa}{L^{3/2}\lambda^{3}}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{\sigma^{3}\kappa^{3}n^{3/2}r}{\lambda^{3}L^{3/2}}+\frac{\sigma^{5}\kappa n^{5/2}r^{5/2}}{\lambda^{5}L^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{4}nr^{2}}{\lambda^{4}L^{3/2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\sigma^{4}n^{2}r^{2}}{\lambda^{4}L^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{4}r^{2}n^{3/2}}{\lambda^{4}L^{3/2}}+\frac{\sigma^{4}\kappa^{2}n^{5/2}r^{2}}{\lambda^{4}L^{5/2}}+\frac{\sigma^{3}n^{3/2}r\kappa}{L^{3/2}\lambda^{3}}.

(70)

We will show that the right hand side of (
70
) is
o\big(\sigma^{2}n\|(\bm{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}}^{\top})^{-1}\|_{F}\big).
Recall that
\|(\bm{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}}^{\top})^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}\gtrsim\frac{r}{L^{2}\lambda^{4}}
. Thus, it suffices to show that multiplying
(
70
)
by
\frac{\lambda^{2}L}{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{nr}}
yields a term that is
o(1)
.

We have

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{2}L}{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{nr}}

\displaystyle\bigg\{\frac{\sigma^{3}\kappa^{3}n^{3/2}r}{\lambda^{3}L^{3/2}}+\frac{\sigma^{5}\kappa n^{5/2}r^{5/2}}{\lambda^{5}L^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{4}nr^{2}}{\lambda^{4}L^{3/2}}\frac{\sigma^{4}n^{2}r^{2}}{\lambda^{4}L^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{4}r^{2}n^{3/2}}{\lambda^{4}L^{3/2}}+\frac{\sigma^{4}\kappa^{2}n^{5/2}r^{2}}{\lambda^{4}L^{5/2}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}n^{3/2}r\kappa}{L^{3/2}\lambda^{3}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\qquad\asymp\frac{\sigma\kappa^{3}n\sqrt{r}}{\lambda\sqrt{L}}+\frac{\sigma^{3}\kappa n^{2}r^{2}}{\lambda^{3}L}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{n}r^{3/2}}{\lambda^{2}\sqrt{L}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}n^{3/2}r^{3/2}}{\lambda^{2}L}+\frac{\sigma^{2}r^{3/2}n}{\lambda^{2}\sqrt{L}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\kappa^{2}n^{2}r^{2}}{\lambda^{2}L^{3/2}}+\frac{\sigma n\sqrt{r}\kappa}{\sqrt{L}\lambda}=o(1),

where the final bound is
o(1)
since
\lambda/\sigma\gg\frac{nr^{2}\kappa^{4}}{\sqrt{L}}
and
L\lesssim n
.

Next, by (
28
),

\displaystyle\big|\|(\bm{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}}^{\top})^{-1}\|_{F}-\|\big(\bm{\mathcal{\widehat{R}}}\bm{\mathcal{\widehat{R}}}^{\top}\big)^{-1}\|_{F}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{l}\big(\bm{\widehat{R}}^{(l)}\big)^{2}\bigg)^{-1}-\mathcal{O}^{\top}\bigg(\sum_{l}\big(\bm{R}^{(l)}\big)^{2}\bigg)^{-1}\mathcal{O}\bigg\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma\kappa\sqrt{nr}}{\lambda\sqrt{L}}\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\lambda^{2}L}+\frac{\sigma\kappa\sqrt{nr}}{\lambda\sqrt{L}}\frac{\sigma^{2}nr^{3/2}}{\lambda^{4}L^{3/2}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}r^{3/2}}{\lambda^{4}L^{3/2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\sigma^{2}nr^{3/2}}{\lambda^{4}L^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}r^{3/2}\sqrt{n}}{\lambda^{4}L^{3/2}}+\frac{\sigma\kappa\sqrt{nr}}{\lambda\sqrt{L}}\frac{\sigma r\sqrt{n}\kappa}{\lambda^{3}L^{2}}+\frac{\sigma\sqrt{nr}\kappa}{L^{3/2}\lambda^{3}}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\bigg|1-\frac{\|\big(\bm{\mathcal{\widehat{R}}}\bm{\mathcal{\widehat{R}}}^{\top}\big)^{-1}\|_{F}}{\|(\bm{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}}^{\top})^{-1}\|_{F}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{L\lambda^{2}}{\sqrt{r}}\bigg(\frac{\sigma\kappa\sqrt{nr}}{\lambda\sqrt{L}}\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\lambda^{2}L}+\frac{\sigma\kappa\sqrt{nr}}{\lambda\sqrt{L}}\frac{\sigma^{2}nr^{3/2}}{\lambda^{4}L^{3/2}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}r^{3/2}}{\lambda^{4}L^{3/2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\sigma^{2}nr^{3/2}}{\lambda^{4}L^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}r^{3/2}\sqrt{n}}{\lambda^{4}L^{3/2}}+\frac{\sigma\kappa\sqrt{nr}}{\lambda\sqrt{L}}\frac{\sigma r\sqrt{n}\kappa}{\lambda^{3}L^{2}}+\frac{\sigma\sqrt{nr}\kappa}{L^{3/2}\lambda^{3}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{\sigma\kappa^{3}\sqrt{n}}{\lambda\sqrt{L}}+\frac{\sigma^{3}\kappa n^{3/2}r^{3/2}}{\lambda^{3}\sqrt{L}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}r}{\lambda^{2}\sqrt{L}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}nr}{\lambda^{2}L}+\frac{\sigma^{2}r\sqrt{n}}{\lambda^{2}\sqrt{L}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\kappa^{2}rn}{\lambda^{2}L^{3/2}}+\frac{\sigma\sqrt{n}\kappa}{\sqrt{L}\lambda}

\displaystyle=o(1),

where the final bound holds since
\lambda/\sigma\gg\frac{nr^{2}\kappa^{4}}{\sqrt{L}}
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix C
Proofs of Lower Bounds

In this section we prove all of our lower bounds. First, in
Section

C.1
we prove
Theorem

3
. In
Section

C.2
we prove the computational lower bound of
Theorem

4
, and finally in
Section

C.3
we prove
Theorem

6
and
Theorem

8
in tandem.

Proof of
Theorem

3

Proof of
Theorem

3
.

The proof is a standard Fano’s inequality argument. Consider the metric space
\mathcal{B}_{n-r,r}=\{\bm{U}:\bm{U}\in\mathbb{O}_{n-r,r}\}
with the metric
\|\bm{U}_{1}\bm{U}_{1}^{\top}-\bm{U}_{2}\bm{U}_{2}^{\top}\|_{F}
. Let
M(a)
denote the packing number of this metric space. By Lemma 5 of
39
, the packing number
M(\sqrt{r}\varepsilon)
of this set for
r\leq n-2r
satisfies

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{c}{\varepsilon}\bigg)^{r(n-r)}\leq M(\sqrt{r}\varepsilon)\leq\bigg(\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\bigg)^{r(n-r)}.

Since
r\leq c\sqrt{n},3r\leq n
for
n
sufficiently large.
By taking
\varepsilon=c/2
, we can find a subset
\mathcal{U}\subset\mathcal{B}_{n-r,r}
with
|\mathcal{U}|\geq 2^{r(n-2r)}
such that for any
\bm{U}_{i}\neq\bm{U}_{j}
in
\mathcal{U}
it holds that

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}_{i}\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}-\bm{U}_{j}\bm{U}_{j}^{\top}\|_{F}\geq\frac{c}{2}\sqrt{r}.

For each
\bm{U}_{i}\in\mathcal{U}
, define
\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{i}
via

\displaystyle\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{1-\delta}\bm{I}_{r}\\
\sqrt{\delta}\bm{U}_{i}\end{pmatrix},

which is an orthonormal matrix. We thus have that if
\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{i}\neq\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{j}
, then

\displaystyle\|\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{i}\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{i}^{\top}-\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{j}\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{j}^{\top}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{2\delta(1-\delta)}\|\bm{U}_{i}-\bm{U}_{j}\|_{F}\geq\sqrt{2\delta(1-\delta)}\inf_{\mathcal{O}:\mathcal{OO}^{\top}=\bm{I}_{r}}\|\|\bm{U}_{i}-\bm{U}_{j}\mathcal{O}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\delta(1-\delta)}\|\bm{U}_{i}\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}-\bm{U}_{j}\bm{U}_{j}^{\top}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c}{2}\sqrt{r\delta(1-\delta)}.

We now let
\bm{S}^{(l)}_{i}=\lambda\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{i}\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{i}^{\top}
for all
l
. Then under
{\sf GOE}
noise with variance
\sigma^{2}
it holds that

\displaystyle KL\bigg(\big\{\bm{S}^{(l)}_{i}\big\},\big\{\bm{S}^{(l)}_{j}\big\}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{L}{2\sigma^{2}}\|\lambda\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{i}\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{i}^{\top}-\lambda\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{j}\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{j}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{L\lambda^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\|\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{i}\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{i}^{\top}-\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{j}\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{j}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\lambda^{2}L}{\sigma^{2}}\|\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{i}-\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{j}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\lambda^{2}L\delta r}{\sigma^{2}}.

Thus, by the generalized Fano’s lemma, it holds that

\displaystyle\inf_{\bm{\widehat{U}}}\sup_{\bm{U}\in\{\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\widehat{U}}\bm{\widehat{U}}^{\top}-\bm{UU}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}\geq c\delta(1-\delta)r\bigg(1-\frac{C\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}L\delta r+\log(2)}{r(n-2r)\log(2)}\bigg).

Since
\|\sin\Theta(\bm{U},\bm{V})\|_{F}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{VV}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}
, by taking
\delta=c_{0}\big(\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{\lambda^{2}L}\wedge 1\big)
for some sufficiently small constant
c_{0}
, we obtain the result.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

4

Proof.

We follow a similar proof to
55
. First, by section A.2 of
40
, we can without loss of generality assume that
\bm{N}^{(l)}
consists of IID
\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
noise and
\sigma^{2}=1
(by dividing through by
\sigma
).

Following
55
, we let the prior distribution for
(\bm{M},\bm{\varepsilon})
be denoted as
\Pi
.
By Theorem 2.6 of
40
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|L_{n}^{\leq D}\|^{2}=1+\mathbb{E}_{\Pi}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor D/2\rfloor}\frac{1}{(2k)!}\langle\bm{\varepsilon}^{(1)},\bm{\varepsilon}^{(2)}\rangle^{2k}\langle\bm{M}^{(1)},\bm{M}^{(2)}\rangle^{2k},

where
(\bm{M}^{(1)},\bm{\varepsilon}^{(1)})
is independent from
(\bm{M}^{(2)},\bm{\varepsilon}^{(2)})
. We note that equation (44) of
55
continues to apply in our setting, yielding

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\langle\bm{\varepsilon}^{(1)},\bm{\varepsilon}^{(2)}\rangle^{2k}={L+k-1\choose k}.

We now note a slight difference from their proof: we have that

\displaystyle\langle\bm{M}^{(1)},\bm{M}^{(2)}\rangle

\displaystyle=\lambda^{2}\langle\bm{u}_{1}\bm{u}_{1}^{\top},\bm{u}_{2}\bm{u}_{2}^{\top}\rangle=\frac{\lambda^{2}}{n^{2}}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}^{(1)}U_{i}^{(2)}\bigg)^{2},

where
U_{i}^{(1)}
and
U_{i}^{(2)}
are two independent collections of Rademachers. However, we note that the product of two Rademacher random variables is again Rademacher, and hence we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg(\sum_{i}U_{i}\bigg)^{4k}\leq n^{2k}(4k-1)!!.

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\langle\bm{M}^{(1)},\bm{M}^{(2)}\rangle^{2k}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda^{4k}}{n^{4k}}\mathbb{E}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}\bigg)^{4k}\leq\frac{\lambda^{4k}}{n^{4k}}n^{2k}(4k-1)!!=\frac{\lambda^{4k}}{n^{2k}}(4k-1)!!.

Thus, we have that

\displaystyle\|L_{n}^{\leq D}\|^{2}=1+\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor D/2\rfloor}\frac{(4k-1)!!}{(2k)!}{L+k-1\choose k}\frac{\lambda^{4k}}{n^{2k}}\leq 1+\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor D/2\rfloor}{L+k-1\choose k}\frac{4^{k}\lambda^{4k}}{n^{2k}}=:1+\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor D/2\rfloor}T_{k}.

We have that

\displaystyle\frac{T_{k+1}}{T_{k}}=\frac{4\lambda^{4}}{n^{2}}\frac{{L+1\choose k+1}}{{L+k-1\choose k}}=\frac{4\lambda^{4}}{n^{2}}\frac{L+k}{k+1}\lesssim\frac{\lambda^{4}L}{n^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{2},

provided that
\lambda^{2}\lesssim\frac{n}{\sqrt{L}}
, which follows from the assumption that
\lambda=o\big(\frac{\sqrt{n}}{L^{1/4}}\big)
. We thus have that

\displaystyle\|L_{n}^{\leq D}\|^{2}\leq 1+O\bigg(\frac{\lambda^{4}L}{n^{2}}\bigg)=1+o(1),

as required.
∎

Proof of
Theorems

6
and
8

Proof.

We will prove both results in tandem. In particular, we will identify a construction of priors
\mathcal{P}_{1}^{*}
and
\mathcal{P}_{2}^{*}
where
\mathcal{P}_{1}^{*}
and
\mathcal{P}_{2}^{*}
lie in
\mathcal{\widetilde{P}}(\lambda_{1})
and
\mathcal{\widetilde{P}}(\lambda_{2})
respectively, and, furthermore, if
\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}
, then both lie in
\mathcal{P}(\lambda_{1})
. Therefore, we focus on
Theorem

8
.

For simplicity, let
\mathcal{P}_{1}=\mathcal{P}(\lambda_{1})
and define
\mathcal{P}_{2}
similarly.
To prove
Theorem

8
we need to prove the two inequalities

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\alpha}^{*}(\mathcal{P}_{1},\mathcal{P}_{2})\geq\frac{\sigma^{2}nr}{L\lambda_{1}^{2}}\frac{\sigma\sqrt{nr}}{\sqrt{L}\lambda_{2}};

(71)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\alpha}^{*}(\mathcal{P}_{1},\mathcal{P}_{2})\geq C\frac{\sigma^{2}nr}{L\lambda_{1}^{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{nr}}.

(72)

We will prove both statements separately under a common framework. Given a set
\mathcal{P}^{*}_{1}\subset\mathcal{P}_{1}
and an alternative hypothesis space
\mathcal{P}_{2}^{*}\subset\mathcal{P}_{2}\subset\mathcal{P}_{1}
, we will show that for all
\bm{U}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}^{*}
it holds that

\displaystyle{\sf Tr}\bigg(\bm{U}_{\perp}^{*}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{*\top}\bm{U}\bm{U}\bigg)=\mu,

for an appropriate choice of
\mu
, where
\bm{U}^{*}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}^{*}
satisfies
\bm{U}^{*\top}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{*}=0
. We further note that

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{U}}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle={\sf Tr}\big(\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{U}}\bm{\widehat{U}}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\perp}\big)={\sf Tr}\bigg(\bm{U}_{\perp}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{U}}\bm{\widehat{U}}^{\top}\bigg).

Therefore, since
\|\sin\Theta(\bm{U},\bm{\widehat{U}})\|_{F}^{2}=\|\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{U}}\|_{F}^{2}
, any confidence interval for
\|\sin\Theta(\bm{U},\bm{\widehat{U}})\|_{F}^{2}
is also a confidence interval for
{\sf Tr}\bigg(\bm{U}_{\perp}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{U}}\bm{\widehat{U}}^{\top}\bigg)
. Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\mu=\|\sin\Theta(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{U}_{1})\|_{F}^{2}.

Let
f_{\pi_{\mathcal{P}_{1}^{*}}}
be the density function for the uniform prior over
\mathcal{P}_{1}^{*}
, with a similar definition for
\mathcal{P}_{2}^{*}
.
By Lemma 1 of
15
it holds that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{{\sf CI}_{\alpha}}(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{1})

\displaystyle\geq\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{P}^{*}_{2}\cup\mathcal{P}^{*}_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}{\sf L}({\sf CI}_{\alpha})\geq|\mu|\bigg(1-2\alpha-{\sf TV}\big(f_{\pi_{\mathcal{P}^{*}_{1}}},f_{\pi_{\mathcal{P}^{*}_{2}}}\big)\bigg)_{+}.

(73)

Therefore, the result is proven if we can lower bound
\mu
and select an appropriate
\mathcal{P}_{1}^{*}
such that
{\sf TV}(f_{\pi_{\mathcal{P}_{1}^{*}}},f_{\pi_{\mathcal{P}_{2}^{*}}})\leq c
, where
c
is a sufficiently small constant. By the inequality

\displaystyle{\sf TV}(f_{1},f_{2})\leq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\chi^{2}(f_{1},f_{2})},

(74)

we can simply upper bound the
\chi^{2}
divergence.

First we consider the inequality (
71
) and give our construction. Since
r\geq 2
, set
r^{\prime}:=\lfloor r/2\rfloor
, and set
r_{0}=r-r^{\prime}
. Let

\displaystyle\bm{W}=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{I}_{r_{0}}\\
\bm{0}_{(n-r_{0})\times r_{0}}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r_{0}}.

In addition, define

\displaystyle\bm{V}^{*}:=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{0}_{r_{0}\times r^{\prime}}\\
\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}\\
\bm{0}_{(n-r)\times r^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r^{\prime}}.

Then set

\displaystyle\bm{U}^{*}:=[\bm{W},\bm{V}^{*}]\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}.

Note that
\bm{U}^{*}
is orthonormal. Now, suppose that
\bm{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{(n-r)\times r^{\prime}}
satisfies
\|\bm{Z}\|_{F}=1
. Set, for some parameter
\rho
to be chosen momentarily,

\displaystyle\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}:=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{0}_{r_{0}\times r^{\prime}}\\
\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}\\
\rho\bm{Z}\end{pmatrix}(\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}+\rho^{2}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z})^{-1/2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r^{\prime}},

and define
\bm{U}_{\bm{Z}}:=[\bm{W},\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}]
, which is orthonormal by construction. Under the null
\mathcal{P}^{*}_{1}
we define

\displaystyle\bm{S}_{*}^{(l)}=\bm{S}_{*}=\lambda^{\prime}\bm{WW}^{\top}+\lambda_{2}\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top},

where
\lambda^{\prime}
is chosen such that
r_{0}(\lambda^{\prime})^{2}+r^{\prime}\lambda_{2}^{2}=r\lambda_{1}^{2}
. We note that in the case
\lambda_{2}=\lambda_{1}
, then
\lambda^{\prime}=\lambda_{1}
, and
\{\bm{S}_{*}^{(l)}\}_{l=1}^{L}
all lie in the parameter space
\mathcal{P}(\lambda_{1})
.
Similarly, we set

\displaystyle\bm{S}_{\bm{Z}}^{(l)}\equiv\bm{S}_{\bm{Z}}=\lambda^{\prime}\bm{WW}^{\top}+\lambda_{2}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}^{\top}.

The matrices
\bm{S}_{*}
and
\bm{S}_{\bm{Z}}
have subspaces
\bm{U}^{*}
and
\bm{U}_{\bm{Z}}
respectively, and both matrices lie in our parameter space. Moreover, since
\bm{W}
is shared, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\sin\Theta(\bm{U}_{\bm{Z}},\bm{U}^{*})\|_{F}^{2}=\|\sin\Theta(\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}},\bm{V}^{*})\|_{F}^{2}={\sf Tr}\big(\rho^{2}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}(\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}+\rho^{2}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z})^{-1}\big).

Since
\|\bm{Z}\|_{F}=1
, it is straightforward to demonstrate that
\frac{\rho^{2}}{1+\rho^{2}}\leq\|\sin\Theta(\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}},\bm{V}^{*})\|_{F}^{2}\leq\rho^{2}
. Assuming that
\rho\ll 1
yields that
\|\sin\Theta(\bm{U}_{\bm{Z}},\bm{U}^{*})\|_{F}^{2}\gtrsim\rho^{2}
.

We let
\pi_{\mathcal{P}^{*}_{2}}
be the uniform prior over matrices
\bm{Z}
satisfying
\|\bm{Z}\|_{F}=1
. We claim that

\displaystyle\chi^{2}(f_{\pi_{\mathcal{P}_{1}^{*}}},f_{\pi_{\mathcal{P}_{2}^{*}}})+1=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{Z},\bm{Z}^{\prime}}\exp\bigg\{\frac{L\lambda_{2}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\langle\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}\bm{V}_{{\bm{Z}}}^{\top}-\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top},\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}^{\top}-\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top}\rangle\bigg\}.

(75)

Assuming this for the moment, we upper bound the right hand side above. We have that

\displaystyle\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{0}&\bm{0}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}&(\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}+\rho^{2}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z})^{-1}&\rho(\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}+\rho^{2}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z})^{-1}\bm{Z}^{\top}\\
\bm{0}&\rho\bm{Z}(\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}+\rho^{2}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z})^{-1}&\rho^{2}\bm{Z}(\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}+\rho^{2}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z})^{-1}\bm{Z}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}

\displaystyle\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}^{\top}-\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{0}&\bm{0}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}&(\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}+\rho^{2}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}&\rho(\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}+\rho^{2}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z})^{-1}\bm{Z}^{\top}\\
\bm{0}&\rho\bm{Z}(\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}+\rho^{2}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z})^{-1}&\rho^{2}\bm{Z}(\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}+\rho^{2}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z})^{-1}\bm{Z}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{0}&\bm{0}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}&\bm{0}&\rho(\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}+\rho^{2}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z})^{-1}\bm{Z}^{\top}\\
\bm{0}&\rho\bm{Z}(\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}+\rho^{2}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z})^{-1}&\bm{0}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}\bm{0}&\bm{0}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}&(\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}+\rho^{2}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}&\bm{0}&\rho^{2}\bm{Z}(\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}+\rho^{2}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z})^{-1}\bm{Z}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle:=\bm{X}_{\bm{Z}}^{(1)}+\bm{X}_{\bm{Z}}^{(2)}.

It therefore holds that

\displaystyle\langle\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}\bm{V}_{{\bm{Z}}}^{\top}-\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top},\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}^{\top}-\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\bm{X}_{\bm{Z}}^{(1)},\bm{X}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}^{(1)}\rangle+\langle\bm{X}_{\bm{Z}}^{(2)},\bm{X}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}^{(2)}\rangle+\langle\bm{X}_{\bm{Z}}^{(1)},\bm{X}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}^{(2)}\rangle+\langle\bm{X}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}^{(1)},\bm{X}_{\bm{Z}}^{(2)}\rangle.

We analyze each term separately. First, we have that

\displaystyle\langle\bm{X}_{\bm{Z}}^{(1)},\bm{X}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}^{(1)}\rangle

\displaystyle=2\rho^{2}{\sf Tr}\bigg[\bm{Z}^{\top}\big(\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}+\rho^{2}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\big)^{-1}\big(\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}+\rho^{2}\bm{Z}^{{}^{\prime}\top}\bm{Z}^{\prime}\big)^{-1}\bm{Z}^{{}^{\prime}\top}\bigg].

Since
\rho\ll 1
and
\|\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\|_{F}\leq 1
, we may write the Neumann series

\displaystyle\big(\bm{I}_{r^{\prime}}+\rho^{2}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\big)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\rho^{2k}(\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z})^{k}.

Plugging this series in yields

\displaystyle\langle\bm{X}_{\bm{Z}}^{(1)},\bm{X}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}^{(1)}\rangle

\displaystyle=2\rho^{2}\langle\bm{Z},\bm{Z}^{\prime}\rangle+R,

where
|R|\leq C\rho^{4}
. Using a similar argument for
\bm{X}_{\bm{Z}}^{(2)}
shows that the remaining terms are all bounded by
C\rho^{4}
. As a result, (
75
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bm{Z},\bm{Z}^{\prime}}\exp\bigg\{\frac{L\lambda_{2}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\langle\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}\bm{V}_{{\bm{Z}}}^{\top}-\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top},\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}^{\top}-\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top}\rangle\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigg\{\frac{CL\lambda_{2}^{2}\rho^{4}}{\sigma^{2}}\bigg\}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{Z},\bm{Z^{\prime}}}\exp\bigg\{\frac{2L\lambda_{2}^{2}\rho^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\langle\bm{Z},\bm{Z}^{\prime}\rangle\bigg\}.

Next, we note that
\bm{Z}
and
\bm{Z}^{\prime}
are matrices drawn uniformly from
\|\bm{Z}\|_{F}=1
; thus, the inner product
\langle\bm{Z},\bm{Z}^{\prime}\rangle
is equivalent to the inner product for two independent uniform random variables of dimension
(n-r)r^{\prime}
. Since the inner product between two independent uniform random variables is subgaussian with
\psi_{2}
norm at most
\frac{C}{\sqrt{(n-r)r^{\prime}}}
, it holds that if
r^{\prime}\asymp r
,

\displaystyle\exp\bigg\{\frac{CL\lambda_{2}^{2}\rho^{4}}{\sigma^{2}}\bigg\}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{Z},\bm{Z^{\prime}}}\exp\bigg\{\frac{2L\lambda_{2}^{2}\rho^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\langle\bm{Z},\bm{Z}^{\prime}\rangle\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigg\{\frac{CL\lambda_{2}^{2}\rho^{4}}{\sigma^{2}}\bigg\}\exp\bigg\{C\frac{L^{2}\lambda_{2}^{4}\rho^{4}}{\sigma^{4}nr}\bigg\}.

Now, take

\displaystyle\rho^{2}

\displaystyle=c_{\rho}\frac{\sigma^{2}nr}{L\lambda_{1}^{2}}\frac{\sigma\sqrt{nr}}{\lambda_{2}\sqrt{L}}.

Plugging this in yields

\displaystyle\chi^{2}(\mathcal{P}_{1}^{*},\mathcal{P}_{2}^{*})+1

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigg\{\frac{Cc_{\rho}^{2}L\lambda_{2}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\bigg(\frac{\sigma^{4}n^{2}r^{2}}{L^{2}\lambda_{1}^{4}}\frac{\sigma^{2}nr}{\lambda_{2}^{2}L}\bigg)\bigg\}+Cc_{\rho}^{2}\frac{L^{2}\lambda_{2}^{4}}{\sigma^{4}nr}\bigg(\frac{\sigma^{4}n^{2}r^{2}}{L^{2}\lambda_{1}^{4}}\frac{\sigma^{2}nr}{\lambda_{2}^{2}L}\bigg)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigg\{Cc_{\rho}^{2}\frac{1}{C_{0}^{4}}+Cc_{\rho}^{2}\frac{1}{C_{0}^{2}}\bigg\},

where the final bound follows since
\lambda_{1}/\sigma\geq C_{0}\frac{nr}{\sqrt{L}}
and
\lambda_{2}\leq\lambda_{1}
. If
C_{0}
is sufficiently large or
c_{\rho}
is sufficiently small, the above is bounded by
1+\varepsilon^{2}
for some small
\varepsilon
.

Similarly, if
\lambda_{2}\geq\frac{nr}{\sqrt{L}}
, we then take
\rho^{2}=c_{\rho}\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{nr}}{L\lambda_{1}^{2}}
to yield

\displaystyle\exp\bigg\{\frac{Cc_{\rho}^{2}L\lambda_{2}^{2}\sigma^{4}nr}{\sigma^{2}\lambda_{1}^{4}L^{2}}\bigg\}\exp\bigg\{Cc_{\rho}^{2}\frac{L^{2}\lambda_{2}^{4}\sigma^{4}nr}{\lambda_{1}^{4}L^{2}nr\sigma^{4}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\exp\bigg\{\frac{Cc_{\rho}^{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}\sigma^{2}nr}{\lambda_{1}^{4}L}\bigg\}\exp\bigg\{Cc_{\rho}^{2}\frac{\lambda_{2}^{4}}{\lambda_{1}^{4}}\bigg\}.

This remains bounded whenever
\lambda_{1}\geq C_{0}\sigma\sqrt{nr/L}
, which it is by assumption (indeed, we assume that
\lambda_{1}/\sigma\gg\frac{nr}{\sqrt{L}}
). This completes the proof of both inequalities provided we can justify (
75
).

We now justify the
\chi^{2}
calculation. Recall that
f_{\pi_{\mathcal{P}_{1}^{*}}}
denotes the joint law of

\{\bm{A}^{(l)}\}_{l=1}^{L}
under the null parameter
\bm{S}_{\ast}^{(l)}
, and for each
\bm{Z}
let

f_{\bm{Z}}
denote the joint law under the alternative parameter
\bm{S}_{\bm{Z}}^{(l)}
. We have that

\displaystyle\bm{S}_{\bm{Z}}^{(l)}-\bm{S}_{*}^{(l)}

\displaystyle==\lambda_{2}\big(\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}^{\top}-\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top}\big).

Let
\pi
be the distribution of
Z
. Then we have that

f_{\pi_{\mathcal{P}_{2}^{*}}}:=\int f_{\bm{Z}}\,d\pi(\bm{Z}).

Hence

\chi^{2}(f_{\pi_{\mathcal{P}_{2}^{*}}},f_{\pi_{\mathcal{P}_{1}^{*}}})+1=\int\int\mathbb{E}_{f_{\pi_{\mathcal{P}_{1}^{*}}}}\left[\frac{f_{\bm{Z}}}{f_{\pi_{\mathcal{P}_{1}^{*}}}}\frac{f_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}}{f_{\pi_{\mathcal{P}_{1}^{*}}}}\right]d\pi(\bm{Z})d\pi(\bm{Z}^{\prime}).

We now compute the inner expectation. For fixed
\bm{Z}
, since the noise is Gaussian,

\displaystyle\frac{f_{\bm{Z}}}{f_{\pi_{\mathcal{P}^{*}}}}

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left\langle\bm{N}^{(l)},\lambda_{2}\big(\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}^{\top}-\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top}\big)\right\rangle-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\|\lambda_{2}\big(\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}^{\top}-\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top}\big)\|_{F}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left\langle\bm{N}^{(l)},\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}^{\top}-\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top}\right\rangle-\frac{L\lambda_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\|\big(\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}^{\top}-\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top}\big)\|_{F}^{2}\right\}.

Multiplying the two likelihood ratios yields

\displaystyle\frac{f_{\bm{Z}}}{f_{\pi_{\mathcal{P}^{*}}}}\frac{f_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}}{f_{\pi_{\mathcal{P}^{*}}}}

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left\langle\bm{N}^{(l)},\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}^{\top}+\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}^{\top}-2\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{L\lambda_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\left(\|\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}^{\top}-\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top}\|_{F}^{2}+\|\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}^{\top}-\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top}\|_{F}^{2}\right)\bigg\}.

It is straightforward to demonstrate that for any matrix
\bm{M}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\langle\bm{N}^{(l)},\bm{M}\rangle\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\exp\bigg\{\frac{\lambda_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\|\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}\bigg\}.

Thus, by independence over
l
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{f_{\pi_{\mathcal{P}^{*}}}}\frac{f_{\bm{Z}}}{f_{\pi_{\mathcal{P}^{*}}}}\frac{f_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}}{f_{\pi_{\mathcal{P}^{*}}}}

\displaystyle=\exp\bigg\{\frac{\lambda_{2}^{2}L}{2\sigma^{2}}\|\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}^{\top}+\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}^{\top}-2\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top}\|_{F}^{2}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{L\lambda_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\bigg(\|\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}^{\top}-\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top}\|_{F}^{2}+\|\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}^{\top}-\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top}\|_{F}^{2}\bigg)\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\exp\bigg\{\frac{\lambda_{2}^{2}L}{2\sigma^{2}}\langle\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}}^{\top}-\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top},\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}\bm{V}_{\bm{Z}^{\prime}}^{\top}-\bm{V}^{*}\bm{V}^{*\top}\rangle\bigg\}.

This completes the proof.
∎
